q^{-n},\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\mathbf{y}\in\mathrm{GL}_{m}(\textbf{p}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}})\setminus\mathrm{GL}_{m}(\textbf{p}^{n+1}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}).\end{cases}

Here
d\mathbf{y}
denotes the unique normalized Haar measure on
\mathbb{G}
. Sometimes it will be convenient to consider the operator

\mathbb{D}^{\alpha}f(\mathbf{x}):=\frac{1-q^{-d}}{1-q^{-(\alpha+d)}}f(\mathbf{x})+\frac{1-q^{\alpha}}{1-q^{-(\alpha+d)}}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\frac{f(\mathbf{x}\star\mathbf{y}^{-1})-f(\mathbf{x})}{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\mathbb{K}}^{\alpha+d}}d\mathbf{y}.

As for any profinite group, the unitary irreducible representations of a compact
p
-adic Lie group
\mathbb{G}
must be smooth functions, and therefore they all have a non-trivial kernel which contains a certain subgroup
\mathbb{G}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where we can assume that
l=l(\xi)
to be the minimum possible. Thus, as a left-inavriant operator, the VT-operator
D^{\alpha}
has the associated symbol:

\displaystyle\sigma_{D^{\alpha}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\xi^{*}(\mathbf{x})D^{\alpha}\xi(x)=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\int_{\mathbb{G}}\frac{\xi(\mathbf{y}^{-1})-I_{d_{\xi}}}{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\mathbb{K}}^{\alpha+d}}d\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{G}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\frac{\xi(\mathbf{y}^{-1})-I_{d_{\xi}}}{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\mathbb{K}}^{\alpha+d}}d\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\Big(-p^{(l-1)(\alpha+d)}p^{-ld}I_{d_{\xi}}-\int_{\mathbb{G}\setminus\mathbb{G}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\frac{I_{d_{\xi}}}{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\mathbb{K}}^{\alpha+d}}d\mathbf{y}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\Big(-p^{l\alpha}p^{-(\alpha+d)}I_{d_{\xi}}-\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}p^{k(\alpha+d)}p^{-kd}(1-p^{-d})I_{d_{\xi}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}p^{l\alpha}\Big(-p^{-(\alpha+d)}-p^{-\alpha}(1-p^{-d})\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}p^{-k\alpha}\Big)I_{d_{\xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}p^{l\alpha}\Big(-p^{-(\alpha+d)}-p^{-\alpha}(1-p^{-d})\frac{1-p^{-l\alpha}}{1-p^{-\alpha}}\Big)I_{d_{\xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}p^{l\alpha}\Big(-p^{-(\alpha+d)}+(1-p^{-d})\frac{1-p^{-l\alpha}}{1-p^{\alpha}}\Big)I_{d_{\xi}}

\displaystyle=\Big(p^{l\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-d}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\Big)I_{d_{\xi}}.

In particular, using the fact we can identify the unitary dual of our groups with a certain subset of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{d}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{d}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
, we get

\sigma_{D^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\Big(\|\xi\|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-d}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+d)}}\Big)I_{d_{\xi}}.

The Vladimirov–Taibleson operator can be considered as a fractional Laplacian for functions on totally disconnected spaces, and it provides a first notion of differentiability. However, for functions of several variables it is natural to consider the differentiability of the function in each variable, or in a certain given direction. For that reason, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be a non-archimedean local field with ring of integers
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}
, prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}=\textbf{p}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}
, and residue field
\mathbb{F}_{q}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}/\textbf{p}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}.
Let
\mathfrak{g}=\spn_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}}\{X_{1},..,X_{d}\}
be a nilpotent
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}
-Lie algebra, and let
\mathbb{G}
be the exponential image of
\mathfrak{g}
, so that
\mathbb{G}
is a compact nilpotent
\mathbb{K}
-Lie group. We will use the symbol symbol
\partial_{X}^{\alpha}
to denote the
directional Vladimirov–Taibleson operator in the direction of
X\in\mathfrak{g}
, or directional VT operator for short, which we define as

\partial_{X}^{\alpha}f(\mathbf{x}):=\frac{1-q^{\alpha}}{1-q^{-(\alpha+1)}}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{K}}}\frac{f(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{exp}(tX)^{-1})-f(\mathbf{x})dt}{|t|_{\mathbb{K}}^{\alpha+1}},\,\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}).

Here
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G})
denotes the space of smooth functions on
\mathbb{G}
, i.e., the collection of locally constant functions with a fixed index of local constancy.

Directional VT operators are interesting because they associate a certain pseudo-differential operator to each element of the Lie algebra. Nonetheless, it is important to remark how this association does not follow the same patter as in the locally connected case, where the correspondence between vectors and operators preserves the Lie algebra structure. However, these operator bear some resemblance to partial derivatives, as we can easily see for the abelian groups
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
. In such context, any analytic vector field
V:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
has an associated symbol
\partial_{V}^{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\partial_{V}^{\alpha}}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot x\}_{p}}\partial_{V}^{\alpha}(e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot x\}_{p}})

\displaystyle=e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot x\}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot(x-tV(x))\}_{p}}-e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot x\}_{p}}}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{e^{2\pi i\{-t(\xi\cdot V(x))\}_{p}}-1}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt=|V(x)\cdot\xi|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}.

In particular, if we define
\partial_{x_{i}}^{\alpha}:=\partial_{\mathbf{e}_{i}}^{\alpha}
, where
\mathbf{e}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq d
are the canonical vectors of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{d}
, then

\sigma_{\partial_{x_{i}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\begin{cases}0,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{i}|_{p}=1,\\
|\xi_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{i}|_{p}>1,\end{cases}

which resembles the symbol of the usual partial derivatives on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, justifying that way our choice of notation. However, we want to be emphatic about the fact that these directional VT operators are not derivatives, but rather some special kind of pseudo-differential operators which we will study with the help of the Fourier analysis on compact groups.

3.
The group
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}

Let
p>2
be a prime number. We will start our work by studying the group
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, or just
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
for simplicity, defined here as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
together with the non-commutative operation

\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y}:=(x_{1}+y_{1},x_{2}+y_{2},x_{3}+y_{3},x_{4}+y_{4}+x_{1}y_{2},x_{5}+y_{5}+x_{1}y_{3}),

where the inverse element ”
\mathbf{x}^{-1}
” of
\mathbf{x}
in the above operation is given by

\mathbf{x}^{-1}=(-x_{1},-x_{2},-x_{3},-x_{4}+x_{1}x_{2},-x_{5}+x_{1}x_{3}).

We can identify this group with the exponential image of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{5,2}
defined by the commutation relations

[X_{1},X_{2}]=X_{4},\quad[X_{1},X_{3}]=[X_{5}].

In the following theorem, the full representation theory of this group is given, together with the essentials on the arguments we will be using along this paper. The idea is simple: while in the real case one only has a single kind on relevant representations, corresponding to the generic orbits of the co-adjoint action, here we need to consider all kind of representations. Indeed, we have those representations which in the real case would correspond to the generic orbits, but here the full dual is going to contain also the representations of
\mathbb{G}/K
, for any closed subgroup
K
, in particular
K=\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G})
, where
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G})
denotes the center of
\mathbb{G}
. Since any nilpotent group can be considered as a sequence of consecutive central extensions, we will always have two kinds of representations: the ”old ones” corresponding to the previous central extensions and the ”new” representations, corresponding to the particular central extension that we are considering. Let us illustrate this with the proof of our results on
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
.

Remark 3.1
.

In this paper, we will identify each equivalence class
\lambda
in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, with its associated representative in the complete system of representatives

\{1\}\cup\big\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}p^{-k}\,:\,\,\text{only finitely many $\lambda_{k}$ are non-zero.}\big\}.

Similarly, every time we consider an element of the quotients
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{-n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
it will be chosen from the complete system of representatives

\{1\}\cup\big\{\sum_{k=n+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}p^{-k}\,:\,\text{only finitely many $\lambda_{k}$ are non-zero}\big\}.

Also, given any
p
-adic number
u
, we will denote by
\vartheta(u)
the
p
-adic valuation of
u\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and for
u\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{d}
we write

\vartheta(u):=\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\vartheta(u_{j}).

Theorem 3.2
.

The unitary dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
can be identified with following subset of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
:

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,\,\text{or},\,\,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

and we can write
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}=A_{1}\cup A_{2}\cup A_{3}
, where

•

A_{1}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1\},

•

A_{2}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\},

•

A_{3}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Moreover, each unitary irreducible representation can be realized in the finite dimensional Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\,\,d_{\xi}:=dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},

acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}).

With this realization and the natural choice of basis for
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, the associated matrix coefficients are going to be

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1}),

and the associated characters are

\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}).

Sometimes we will use the notation

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\,:\,h,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2}).

Proof.

Notice how the center of
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
, here denoted by
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})
, is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}
. If a finite-dimensional continuous representation
\pi
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
is trivial on
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})
, then it must descend to a representation of the abelian group
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}/\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
. In this way, we obtain the one dimensional representations

\pi_{\xi}(x)=\chi_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},1,1)}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{3},

and these can be indexed by the set

A_{1}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1\}.

On the other hand, if a representation is not trivial on the center, i.e. if
\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1
, we need to consider the non-commutative representations

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}),

realized in the finite-dimensional sub-spaces of
L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\,\,d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

Notice how:

•

The representation space
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
is invariant under the action of
\pi_{\xi}
, since

\displaystyle\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u+t)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})t\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}+t)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u),

for any
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
|t|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}.

•

The linear operator
\pi_{\xi}
is indeed a representation of
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
, since

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}(\textbf{y})\varphi(u)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})(e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}y_{2}+\xi_{5}y_{3})u\}_{p}}\varphi(u+y_{1}))

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u\}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}y_{2}+\xi_{5}y_{3})(u+x_{1})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+y_{1}+x_{1})

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}(x_{1}+y_{1})+\xi_{2}(x_{2}+y_{2}))+\xi_{3}(x_{3}+y_{3})+\xi_{4}(x_{4}+y_{4}+y_{1}x_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{5}+y_{5}+y_{1}x_{3})+(\xi_{4}(x_{2}+y_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{3}+y_{3}))u\}_{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\varphi(u+y_{1}+x_{1})

\displaystyle=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})\varphi(u).

Clearly,
\pi_{\xi}
also defines an unitary operator since

\pi^{*}_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)=e^{2\pi i\{-\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}-(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(u-x_{1})\}_{p}}\varphi(u-x_{1})=\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}^{-1})\varphi(u).

Let us check these are indeed all the desired representations. To this end we write

\varphi_{h}(u):=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u),\quad\,\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

This collection of functions defines an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, and the associated matrix coefficients are:

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle:=(\pi_{\xi}\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u\}_{p}}\varphi_{h}(u+x_{1})\overline{\varphi}_{h^{\prime}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\int_{h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1}).

With this we can compute

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})h\}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}),

and from the above it is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,2}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{2}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})dx

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})dx_{2}dx_{3}.

Now we consider cases. First, if
|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},
we get that

\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})=\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{3})=\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{3}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2}),

so that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})dx_{2}dx_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{3}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})dx_{2}dx_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{-1}dx_{3}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{-1}.

Similarly, if
|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{4}|
then

\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})=\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3}+\frac{\xi_{4}}{\xi_{5}}x_{2})=\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{4}}{\xi_{5}}x_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2}),

so that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})dx_{2}dx_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{4}}{\xi_{5}}x_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})dx_{2}dx_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{-1}dx_{2}=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{-1}.

In any case,
\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,2}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}=1
, proving how each
\pi_{\xi}
defines an unitary irreducible representation. Next, we need to use the obtained characters to count the number of different equivalence classes of representations, by counting the number of different characters obtained. Once again we need to consider two cases. First, if
|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},
then the condition
\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}=a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
implies that

x_{2}=\frac{a}{\xi_{4}}-\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{3},\,\implies\,\,\xi_{2}x_{2}=\frac{\xi_{2}}{\xi_{4}}a-\frac{\xi_{5}\xi_{2}}{\xi_{4}}x_{3}.

This shows how

e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+\xi_{2}x_{2}+\xi_{3}x_{3}\}_{p}}=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+(\xi_{3}+\frac{\xi_{5}\xi_{2}}{\xi_{4}})x_{3}\}_{p}},\quad\text{for}\,\,|\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p},

so that we only need to consider
\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and thus, these representations are indexed by the set

A_{2}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

In a very similar way, if
|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p},
then the condition
\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}=a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
implies that

x_{3}=\frac{a}{\xi_{5}}-\frac{\xi_{4}}{\xi_{5}}x_{2},\,\implies\,\,\xi_{3}x_{3}=\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{5}}a-\frac{\xi_{4}\xi_{2}}{\xi_{5}}x_{2},

showing how

e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+\xi_{2}x_{2}+\xi_{3}x_{3}\}_{p}}=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+(\xi_{2}-\frac{\xi_{4}\xi_{2}}{\xi_{5}})x_{2}\}_{p}},\quad\text{for}\,\,|\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p},

so that we only need to count
\xi_{3}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and thus these representations are indexed by the set

A_{3}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{4}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Finally, in order to prove these are indeed all the desired representations, let us define

•

A_{1}(n):=\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1\},

•

A_{2}(n):=\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}
,

•

A_{3}(n):=\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{4}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\},

•

B_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}}(n)=A_{1}(n)\cup A_{2}(n)\cup A_{3}(n)\subset p^{-n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5},

where each of the sets
A_{j}(n)
,
j=1,2,3,
are subsets of the
p
-adic ball
B_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}}(n)
. With this notation

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi\in B_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}}(n)}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi\in A_{1}(n)}1^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{2}(n)}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{3}(n)}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}1^{2}+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4},)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{3}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5},)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}>1,\,\,1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{2}|\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{3n}+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}}(\frac{p^{2n}}{|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}})(p^{n})|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}(\frac{p^{2n}}{|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}})(p^{n})|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{3n}+p^{3n}\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}1=p^{3n}+p^{3n}(p^{2n}-1)

\displaystyle=p^{5n}=|\mathbb{G}^{5,2}/\mathbb{G}^{5,2}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})|.

This concludes the proof.
∎

With the above theorem, functions
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})
have the Fourier series representation

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{\xi_{3}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{\xi_{2}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}}\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)],

where the Fourier transform
\widehat{f}(\xi)
is the linear operator defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)\varphi(u)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,2}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(u-x_{1})\}_{p}}\varphi(u-x_{1})d\mathbf{x},\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}.

Alternatively, by using our expressions for the matrix coefficients, we can see this operator as the matrix
\widehat{f}(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}^{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\times\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}
defined entry wise by the expression

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,2}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})_{hh^{\prime}}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})d\mathbf{x},\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi},

which in terms of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
-Fourier transform is expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h,\xi_{4},\xi_{5})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})]*_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h,\xi_{4},\xi_{5}).

With this Fourier series representation, a linear invariant operator
T
can be written as a pseudo-differential operator

\displaystyle Tf(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}}\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\sigma_{T}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)],

where the symbol is defined as the mapping

\sigma_{T}(\xi):=\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})T\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{C}^{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\times\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{4})\|_{p}},\quad\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2},

and we can use this association of symbols to study the particular example of the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian, which we can easily check defines an left invariant operator. We know how
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
is a stratified group since
\mathfrak{g}^{5,2}=V_{1}\oplus V_{2}
, where

V_{1}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\{X_{1},X_{2},X_{2}\},\quad V_{2}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\{X_{3},X_{4}\},

so that, given any collection
\textbf{W}:=\{W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}
} with

W_{i}=w_{i}^{1}X_{1}+w_{i}^{2}X_{2}+w_{i}^{3}X_{3},

which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{5,2}/[\mathfrak{g}^{5,2},\mathfrak{g}^{5,2}]
, we can define its associated Vladimirov sub-Laplacian as

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}f(x):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{2}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{3}})f(x),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2}).

For
\alpha>0
the following theorem proves how Conjecture
Conjecture
holds true for this operator on
\mathbb{G}^{5,2}
, and give a particular version of the first par of Theorem
1.7
.

Remark 3.3
.

In every section of this work we are dedicated to the proof of two results: one about the representation theory of a certain compact group, like Theorems
1.3
,
2.6
and
3.2
, and a second, which actually is a corollary of the first, about the spectrum of the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian. For this reason, we will often be reusing arguments and the reader will notice a lot of similarities from section to section, because we are essentially proving the same results again and again, each time on a different group. Sill, the author decided to repeat some of our arguments in each section aiming to make possible to read them independently. The author sincerely hopes this is not bothersome to the reader.

Theorem 3.4
.

The Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
assocated with the collection
W
is a globally hypoelliptic operator, which is invertible in the space of mean zero functions. Moreover, the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})
can be written as the direct sum

L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,2})=\overline{\bigoplus_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2}}\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}},\,\,\,\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

where each finite-dimensional sub-space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

is an invariant sub-space of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, and its spectrum restricted to
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}
is given by

Spec(\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}})=\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{k}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\,:\,1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\Big\},

so that
Spec(\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha})
is going to be the collection of real numbers

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{k}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}},

where
\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2},\,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
, and the corresponding eigenfunctions are given by

\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{\mathbf{x}}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})+\tau x_{1}\}_{p}},\,\,\,\,\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,2},\,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

Proof.

To prove our theorem, and this will be a recurrent argument along this paper, we simply need to consider the subspaces

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}.

An alternative description of this space could be

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathcal{D}_{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})\}_{p}}f\,:\,f(x_{1}+u)=f(x_{1}),\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,|u|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}\},

and in this way it is not hard to see how the following set defines an orthonormal basis for the space:

\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3})(h^{\prime})+\tau x_{1}\}_{p}},\quad 1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

With this we can check how

\displaystyle\partial_{W_{i}}^{\alpha}\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{e^{2\pi i\{-t(\xi_{1}w_{i}^{1}+(\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4})w_{i}^{2}+(\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})w_{i}^{3})\}_{p}}-1}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}),

so that
\partial_{W_{i}}^{\alpha}\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x})=

\begin{cases}\Big(|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}),&\,\text{if}\,|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}>1,\\
0,&\,\text{if}\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.\end{cases}

Thus we can write

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x})=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}).

Finally, in order to prove the global hypoellipticity, let us recall for the reader the arguments in
[
18
]
. Given any compact group, if a linear operator
T
is a translation invariant operator, then it can be written as

Tf(x)=\sum_{\xi\in\widehat{G}}d_{\xi}Tr[\xi(x)\sigma_{T}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)].

Using this symbolic representation we can see how, for the particular case of a compact Lie group
G
, the global hypoellipticity becomes equivalent to two conditions: first, the invertibility of the symbol
\sigma_{T}(\xi)
, for all but finitely many
\xi\in\widehat{G}
, and second, the operator norm of the inverse of the symbol should be polynomially bounded. The reason is how smooth functions are characterized via their Fourier coefficients as the functions with rapidly decreasing Fourier transform. In our case the same is true, and we have the direct sum decomposition for each reprsentation space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

so that for the particula case of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, it should be clear that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{op}=\max_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{op},

and

\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)^{-1}\|_{op}^{-1}=\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{inf}=\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{inf}=\min_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{inf},

so, we can conclude that

\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{op}\leq\|\xi\|_{p}^{\alpha},\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\|_{p}^{\alpha}\leq\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{inf}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

4.
The group
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}

Let
p>3
be a prime number. In this section we consider the group
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, or just
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
for simplicity, defined here as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
together with the non-commutative operation

\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y}:=(x_{1}+y_{1},x_{2}+y_{2},x_{3}+y_{3},x_{4}+y_{4}+x_{1}y_{2},x_{5}+y_{5}+x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{2}),

and inverse element

\mathbf{x}^{-1}:=(-x_{1},-x_{2},-x_{3},-x_{4}+x_{1}x_{2},-x_{5}+x_{1}x_{4}+x_{2}x_{3}-\frac{1}{2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}).

We can identify this group with the exponential image of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{5,3}
defined by the commutation relations

[X_{1},X_{2}]=X_{4},\quad[X_{1},X_{4}]=X_{5},\quad[X_{2},X_{3}]=X_{5}.

In Theorem
4.3
, in a similar way as for the previous groups, we will provide an explicit description of the unitary dual of
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
. This time, before stating the theorem and proceeding with its proof, we will establish the following auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}dx_{2}dx_{4}=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}.

Proof.

Define the auxiliary function

f_{\xi_{4},\xi_{5}}(x_{2},x_{4}):=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u\}_{p}}du.

Then, using its Fourier transform to calculate its
L^{2}
-norm, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}[f_{\xi_{4},\xi_{5}}](\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}u-\alpha)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}u-\beta)du

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\alpha}{\xi_{4}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\mathds{1}_{\frac{\beta}{\xi_{5}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Big(\big[\frac{\alpha}{\xi_{4}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\cap\big[\frac{\beta}{\xi_{5}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\Big),

where
\mu_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denotes the normalized Haar measure on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. In this way its is clear how

\displaystyle\|f_{\xi_{4},\xi_{5}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\alpha,\beta)\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}}\mu_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Big(\big[\frac{\alpha}{\xi_{4}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\cap\big[\frac{\beta}{\xi_{5}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\max\{|\xi_{4}|_{p},|\xi_{5}|_{p}\}^{-1}\sum_{(\alpha,\beta)\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}}\mu_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Big(\big[\frac{\alpha}{\xi_{4}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\cap\big[\frac{\beta}{\xi_{5}}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big]\big)

\displaystyle=\max\{|\xi_{4}|_{p},|\xi_{5}|_{p}\}^{-1}\mu_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

There is an alternative proof for the previous lemma, which is observing how

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u\}_{p}}du=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4}),

but we prefer here the previous proof because it introduce the argument we will use later for more complicated integrals, like
p
-adic Gaussians.

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 4.3
.

The unitary dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,3}
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
can be identified with the following subset of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
:

\mathbb{G}^{5,3}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,\,(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Moreover, each unitary irreducible representation can be realized in the finite dimensional Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}:=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}(u_{1}x_{2})+\xi_{5}(-x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+(x_{1},x_{2})),

where

d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}=\begin{cases}|\xi_{4}|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1,\\
|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2},\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}=1.\end{cases}

With this realization and the natural choice of basis for
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, the associated matrix coefficients are going to be

(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\xi_{5}x_{3}(h^{\prime}_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}h_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2}),

and the associated characters are
\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})=

\begin{cases}|\xi_{4}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(x_{1},x_{2}),\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\\
|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{4}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}),\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}=1,\\
|\xi_{4}|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})\,\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}.\end{cases}

Sometimes we will use the notation

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\,:\,h,h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}\},

where

I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-3\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\\
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let us denote by
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})
the center of
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
, which in this case is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. If a finite-dimensional continuous representation
\pi
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
is trivial on
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})
, then it must descend to a representation of
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}/\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\mathbb{H}_{1}
, and these where already described in Theorem
1.3
, where it is proven that

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\mathbb{H}_{1}}=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\times\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\cong\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{4}\,:\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\},

and the associated representations are given by

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}(ux_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}),\,\,\,\,\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi},

where

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\spn_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\,\,\,\varphi_{h}(u):=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}}(u),\,\,\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=|\xi_{4}|_{p}.

The characters associated to these representations have the form

\chi_{\pi_{\xi_{3}}}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+{\xi_{4}}(hx_{2})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})=|\xi_{4}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2}),

thus the irreducibility of
[\pi_{\xi}]
is proved by the condition

\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,3}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}\,=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}|\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}\,=1.

Alternatively, if a representation is not trivial on the center, then we must have
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
, and we can realize these in the finite dimensional Hilbert spaces

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}:=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},

where

d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p},\quad I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},

acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+(x_{1},x_{2})).

To see how this is indeed a representation, let us define the polynomial

P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)=\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2}),

so that

\displaystyle P_{\xi}(\mathbf{y},u+(x_{1},x_{2}))

\displaystyle=\xi\cdot\mathbf{y}+\xi_{4}x_{1}y_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{1}y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{2}+x_{2}y_{3})

\displaystyle\quad+\xi_{4}y_{2}u_{1}+\xi_{5}((y_{4}+x_{1}y_{2})u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}u_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{3}u_{2})

proving how

P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}(\mathbf{y},u+(x_{1},x_{2}))=P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\mathbf{y},u),\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{y})\varphi(u)=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\mathbf{y})\varphi(u).

Let us simplify our notation by writing
K_{\xi}:=p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.
We can compute

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}))_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h}(u+(x_{1},x_{2}))\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\int_{h^{\prime}+K_{\xi}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}h_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}+x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})+x_{3}(h^{\prime}_{2}))\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2}),

and from this it readily follows that

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in I_{\xi}}\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\int_{h^{\prime}+K_{\xi}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}u_{1}^{2})\}_{p}}du_{1}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}.

Finally we have two cases. If
|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}
then

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{5}x_{4}u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+\xi_{2}x_{2}+\xi_{3}x_{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{4}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}),

so that
\xi_{4}
disappears and we see how we only need to count those characters indexed by

(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{3}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Notice how these are all irreducible since

\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,3}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{4}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{4}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})d\mathbf{x}=1.

In the case when
|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}
,

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}}(x_{3})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{|p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4}),

so the desired representations are indexed by

(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,\xi_{3}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},

and these are irreducible because

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,3}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{4})dx_{2}dx_{4}=1,

where the last equality comes as an application of Lemma
4.1
. To conclude our proof, we define the sets

•

A_{1}(n):=\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\,\xi_{3}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}.

•

A_{2}(n):=

\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>1\}.

•

A_{3}(n):=\{\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\,:\,1=|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{3}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}\}.

The representations obtained are all the desired representations since

\displaystyle\sum_{[\pi_{\xi}]\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,3}\cap(\mathbb{G}^{5,3}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\bot}}

\displaystyle d_{\xi}^{2}=\sum_{\xi\in A_{1}(n)\cup A_{2}(n)\cup A_{3}(n)}d_{\xi}^{2}=\sum_{\xi\in A_{1}(n)}d_{\xi}^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{2}(n)}d_{\xi}^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{3}(n)}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{3}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}(|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(|\xi_{4}|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p})^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{3n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{\xi_{4}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{3n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}+p^{3n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\frac{p^{n}-|\xi_{5}|_{p}}{|\xi_{5}|_{p}}|\xi_{5}|_{p}

\displaystyle=p^{5n}=|\mathbb{G}^{5,3}/\mathbb{G}^{5,3}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})|.

With this, the proof is concluded.
∎

According to the above theorem, functions
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})
have the Fourier series representation

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}+\sum_{\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{-1}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)],

where the Fourier transform
\widehat{f}(\xi)
is the linear operator defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)\varphi(u)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,3}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}((u_{1}-x_{1})x_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(u_{1}-x_{1})+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(u_{1}-x_{1})^{2}+x_{3}(u_{2}-x_{2}))\}_{p}}\varphi(u-(x_{1},x_{2})),d\mathbf{x},\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}.

Alternatively, by using our expressions for the matrix coefficients we can see this operator as the matrix
\widehat{f}(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}^{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}\times\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}}
defined by the expression

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,3}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})_{hh^{\prime}}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}x_{2}(h_{1})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}h_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1})^{2}+x_{2}(h_{1})+x_{3}(h_{2}))\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})d\mathbf{x},

where
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, which in terms of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
-Fourier transform is expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h_{1}+\xi_{5}(\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}+h_{1}),\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}]*_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h_{1}+\xi_{5}(\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}+h_{1}),\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{5}).

With this Fourier series representation, a linear invariant operator
T
can be written as a pseudo-differential operator

\displaystyle T_{\sigma}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\sigma_{T}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad\sum_{\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{-1}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\sigma_{T}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)],

Notice how this time
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
is not an stratified group. Still, given any collection
\textbf{W}:=\{W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}
} with

W_{i}=w_{i}^{1}X_{1}+w_{i}^{2}X_{2}+w_{i}^{3}X_{3},

which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{5,3}/[\mathfrak{g}^{5,3},\mathfrak{g}^{5,3}]
, we can define its associated Vladimirov sub-Laplacian as

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}f(x):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{2}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{3}})f(x),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3}).

For
\alpha>0
the following theorem proves how our conjecture for this operator holds true on
\mathbb{G}^{5,3}
too, even when we lack here the graded or stratified structure

Theorem 4.4
.

The Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
associated with the collection
W
is a globally hypoelliptic operator, which is invertible in the space of mean zero functions. Moreover, the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})
can be written as the direct sum

L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,3})=\overline{\bigoplus_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,3}}\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}},\,\,\,\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

where each finite-dimensional sub-space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},

is an invariant sub-space of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, and its spectrum restricted to
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}
is given by

Spec(\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1}+\tau_{1},\xi_{2}+\tau_{2}+\frac{1}{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\xi_{5},\xi_{3}+(h_{2}^{\prime})\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}},

and the corresponding eigenfunctions are given by

\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\xi_{5}x_{3}(h^{\prime}_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}h_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2})+\tau\cdot(x_{1},x_{2})\}_{p}},

where
1\leq|\tau_{1}|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},\,\,1\leq|\tau_{2}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}.

Proof.

As we anticipated, we will be following the same arguments as in Theorem
3.4
. We focus again on the sub-spaces

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\}.

By Theorem
1.3
, an alternative description of this space could be

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}u_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}+x_{3}(h_{2}^{\prime}))\}_{p}}\mathcal{D}_{\xi_{4},\xi_{5}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2})

\displaystyle=\{e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}(h_{1}^{\prime})x_{2}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}+x_{3}(h_{2}^{\prime}))\}_{p}}f\,:\,f((x_{1},x_{2})+u)=f(x_{1},x_{2}),\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,u\in K_{\xi}\},

where

K_{\xi}:=:=p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

In this way, it is not hard to see how the following set defines an orthonormal basis for the space:

\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{4}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\xi_{5}x_{3}(h^{\prime}_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}h_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2})+\tau\cdot(x_{1},x_{2})\}_{p}},

where
1\leq|\tau_{1}|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},\,\,1\leq|\tau_{2}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}.

With this we can check how

\displaystyle\partial_{W_{i}}^{\alpha}\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{e^{2\pi i\{-t((\xi_{1}+\tau_{1})w_{i}^{1}+(\xi_{2}+h_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+\tau_{2})w_{i}^{2}+(\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h_{2}^{\prime})w_{i}^{3})\}_{p}}-1}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}),

so that

\displaystyle\partial_{W_{i}}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x})=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}\setminus\mathbb{Z}_{p}}((\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h^{\prime}\xi_{4},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i})

\displaystyle\times\Big(|(\xi_{1}+\tau,\xi_{2}+h_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+\tau_{2},\xi_{3}+h^{\prime}\xi_{5})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}).

Thus we can write

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1}+\tau,\xi_{2}+h_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+\tau_{2},\xi_{3})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}).

Finally, in order to prove the global hypoellipticity of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, it should be clear that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{op}=\max_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{op},

and

\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{inf}=\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{inf}=\min_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\|\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{inf},

so, we can conclude that

\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{op}\leq\|\xi\|_{p}^{\alpha},\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\|_{p}^{\alpha}\leq\|\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{inf}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

5.
The Cartan group
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}

Let
p>3
be a prime number. In this section we consider the group
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, or just
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
for simplicity, defined here as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
together with the non-commutative operation

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y}

\displaystyle:=(x_{1}+y_{1})X_{1}+(x_{2}+y_{2})X_{2}+(x_{3}+y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})X_{3}

\displaystyle+(x_{4}+y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{2}+x_{1}y_{3})X_{4}+(x_{5}+y_{5}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}y_{2}^{2}+x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}x_{2}y_{2})X_{5},

with associated inverse element

\mathbf{x}^{-1}=(-x_{1},-x_{2},-x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2},-x_{4}+x_{1}x_{3}-x_{2}\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2},-x_{5}+x_{2}x_{3}-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2}x_{1}).

We can identify this group with the exponential image of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{5,4}
defined by the commutation relations

[X_{1},X_{2}]=X_{3},\quad[X_{1},X_{3}]=X_{4},\quad[X_{2},X_{3}]=X_{5}.

Some authors call this algebra
the Cartan algebra
, and to its corresponding exponential image
the Cartan group

\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
. In the following theorem, we will provide an explicit description of the unitary dual of the Cartan group over the
p
-adic integers.

Remark 5.1
.

Recall how, given any
p
-adic number
\lambda
, the symbol
\vartheta(\lambda)
denotes the
p
-adic valuation of
\lambda
. If
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},...,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{d}
then

\vartheta(\lambda)=\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\vartheta(\lambda_{j}).

Theorem 5.2
.

The unitary dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,4}
of the compact Cartan group
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
can be identified with the union of the five following disjoint subsets of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
:
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,4}=A_{1}\cup A_{2}\cup A_{3}\cup A_{4}\cup A_{5}
, where

A_{1}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p}\,\wedge\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1},\,\text{or}\,|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1\,\wedge\,\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

A_{2}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:|\xi_{4}|_{p}=1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{3}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

A_{3}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\,\,\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

A_{4}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\},

A_{5}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{3}|_{p},\,\,(\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Moreover, if we define the polynomials
P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u):=

\begin{cases}\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{3}u),\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{1},\\
\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}u^{2})+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}+x_{3})u,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1,\end{cases}

then each unitary irreducible representation can be realized in the finite dimensional Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}:=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}

where
d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
, and the representation acts according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=\begin{cases}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}),\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{1},\\
e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}\varphi\big(u+\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big),\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{2}\cup A_{3}\cup A_{4}\cup A_{5}.\end{cases}

With the previous realization, and the natural choice of basis for
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, the associated matrix coefficients are going to be

(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=\begin{cases}e^{2\pi i\{P_{1}(\xi,\mathbf{x},h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1}),\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{1},\\
e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\big(\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big),\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{2}\cup A_{3}\cup A_{4}\cup A_{5},\end{cases}

and the associated characters are
\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x}):=

\begin{cases}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,\xi\in A_{1},\\
\frac{\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}}{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}}\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1.\end{cases}

Sometimes we will use the notation

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\,:\,h,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}.

Proof.

Let us start by the level one representations, that is, those who are trivial on
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(p^{1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and must therefore descend to a representation of the finite group of Lie type
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}/\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(p^{1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
. For this group, the Kirillov orbit method gives us
4
kind of co-adjoint orbits, and therefore
4
different kinds of representations, which we can realize in the following way:

(1)

First we have the
0
-dimensional orbits, corresponding to the characters of the abelian group
\mathbf{exp}(\mathfrak{g}^{5,4}/[\mathfrak{g}^{5,4},\mathfrak{g}^{5,4}])
, which are given by

\chi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})=\chi_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},1,1,1)}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}},\quad\|(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\|_{p}\leq p.

(2)

Second, we have those representations which are trivial on the center of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
. If a finite-dimensional continuous representation
\pi
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
is trivial on
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4})
, then it must descend to a representation of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}/\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4})\cong\mathbb{H}_{1}
, and these were already described in Theorem
1.3
, where it is proven that

\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1}=\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},1,1)\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\},

and the associated representations are given by

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)=\pi_{(1,1,\xi_{3},1,1)}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{3}(x_{3}+ux_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}),\,\,\,\,\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi},\quad\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p.

(3)

The third possibility is that the representation is not trivial on the center, but it is trivial on the variable
x_{5}
. In such case, the representation descends to a representation of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}/\mathbf{exp}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}X_{5})\cong\mathcal{B}_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and we discussed already how these have the form

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)=\pi_{(1,\xi_{2},1,\xi_{4},1)}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{2}x_{2}+\xi_{4}(x_{4}+ux_{3}+\frac{u^{2}}{2}x_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}),\quad\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p.

(4)

For the fourth kind of co-adjoint orbit we can use the realization in
[
22
, p.p. 13]
:

\displaystyle\quad\quad\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)

\displaystyle=\pi_{(\xi_{1},1,1,\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)

\displaystyle:=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}u^{2}+3\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}^{2}u)+x_{3}u\}_{p}}\varphi(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}),

where the representation space is given by

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}:=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,:\,h\in p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}.

More generally, we can consider
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
as an extension of the group
\mathcal{B}_{4}
, which is itself an extension of
\mathbb{H}_{1}
, so the representation theory of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
should also include that of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
, which are precisely those representations indexed by the set
A_{1}
. See again Theorem
2.6
and
[
28
]
for more details. On the other hand, if a representation is not trivial on the variable
x_{5}
, then we must have
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
, and we can realize these in the finite dimensional Hilbert spaces

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}^{\prime}:=p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}(\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2}x_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{1}\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2})+\frac{1}{6\xi_{5}}(3x_{1}u^{2}+3\xi_{4}x_{1}^{2}u+\xi_{4}^{2}x_{1}^{3})+x_{3}u\}_{p}}\varphi(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}).

To simplify our notation let us write

P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u):=\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}u^{2}+3\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}^{2}u)+x_{3}u.

Then we can see how
\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}
is indeed a representation since:

\displaystyle P_{\xi}^{\prime}

\displaystyle(\textbf{y},u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})=\xi\cdot\mathbf{y}+\xi_{3}x_{1}y_{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}y_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}y_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}y_{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}y_{2}^{3})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}y_{2}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})^{2}+3\xi_{5}^{2}y_{2}^{2}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))+y_{3}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})

\displaystyle=\xi\cdot\mathbf{y}+\xi_{3}x_{1}y_{2}+\xi_{5}x_{1}\frac{y_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}y_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}y_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}y_{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}y_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}y_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}y_{2}(u^{2}+2(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})u+(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})^{2})+3\xi_{5}^{2}y_{2}^{2}u)+y_{3}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})=\xi\cdot\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle+\xi_{3}x_{1}y_{2}+\xi_{4}(x_{1}y_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}\frac{y_{2}^{2}}{2})+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}y_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}y_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}y_{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}y_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}y_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}y_{2}(u^{2}+(\xi_{4}^{2}x_{1}^{2}+2\xi_{4}\xi_{5}x_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}^{2}))+3\xi_{5}^{2}(y_{2}^{2}+2x_{2}y_{2})u)+(y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})u,

which we can be rewritten as

\displaystyle P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\textbf{y},u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})=\xi\cdot\mathbf{y}+\xi_{3}x_{1}y_{2}+\xi_{4}(x_{1}y_{3}+\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2}y_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}\frac{y_{2}^{2}}{2}+x_{1}x_{2}y_{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}y_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}y_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}y_{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}y_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}y_{2}^{2}+\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{2}y_{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}y_{2}u^{2}+3\xi_{5}^{2}(y_{2}^{2}+2x_{2}y_{2})u)+(y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})u,

and from this we obtain

P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\textbf{y},u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})=P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y},u),
hence

\displaystyle\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\textbf{y})\varphi(u)=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})(e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\textbf{y},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\textbf{y},u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+x_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+x_{2})

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+\xi_{4}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})_{1}+\xi_{5}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})_{2})

\displaystyle=\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})\varphi(u).

It is not hard to see how this representation defines an unitary operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}^{\prime}
, since

\displaystyle-P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))=\xi\cdot-\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{1}x_{2}-\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}(u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))^{2}+3\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}^{2}(u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})))-x_{3}(u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})),

thus

\displaystyle-P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))=\xi\cdot-\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{1}y_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{2}y_{3}-\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}(u-(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))(u-\xi_{4}x_{1}))-x_{3}u

\displaystyle=\xi\cdot-\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{1}x_{2}+\xi_{4}(x_{1}y_{3}-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2}x_{2})+\xi_{5}(x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2})-\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}x_{2}^{3}+3\xi_{5}x_{2}(u^{2}-\xi_{5}x_{2}u))+(-x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2})u=P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}^{-1},u).

By using the natural choice of basis for this space, we can compute

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}))_{hh^{\prime}}=(\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{h^{\prime}+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du.

From this it readily follows that

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{h\in I_{\xi}^{\prime}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{h+\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du.

We can use the function with two purposes. First to check how these representations are indeed irreducible, and second, we can also count the number of different non-equivalent classes of unitary irreducible representations, by counting the number of different functions among these characters. We need to consider cases.

Case 1:
When
|\xi_{4}|_{p}=1
but
|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{3}|_{p}
then

\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\xi_{3}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du,

so that we can rewrite

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\xi_{3}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}u^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}^{2}u+\xi_{5}x_{3}u+\xi_{3}\xi_{5}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}u^{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}u+\xi_{3}\xi_{5}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du.

This is the character of an irreducible representation since

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,4}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}^{2}x_{2}u^{2}+\xi_{5}x_{3}u+\xi_{3}\xi_{5}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}x_{2}u^{2}+(\xi_{5}x_{3}+\xi_{3}x_{2})u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}=1,

where we used Lemma
2.3
above. Notice how the appearance of the function
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{5}x_{2})
implies that we must choose
\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. These are precisely the representations indexed by the set
A_{2}
.

Case 2:
If it happens that
|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1
and
|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}
, then we know how:

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=|\xi_{4}|_{p}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\frac{\xi_{5}^{2}}{6\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{3}\}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}\xi_{4}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du.

To see how this is the character of an irreducible representation, define the auxiliary functions

f(x_{2},x_{3})=\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}\xi_{4}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du\Big|,\quad F(x_{2},x_{3},y):=\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}\xi_{4}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}y+\xi_{4}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du\Big|,

so that
F(x_{1},x_{2},x_{2})=f(x_{1},x_{2})
, and

\inf_{y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}F(x_{2},x_{3},y)^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}\leq\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}f(x_{2},x_{3})^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}\leq\inf_{y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}F(x_{2},x_{3},y)^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}.

however, by Lemma
2.4
, for any
y
we have

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}F(x_{2},x_{3},y)^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{-1}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}f(x_{2},x_{3})^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}.

With this argument we can prove that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,4}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\Big|e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}\xi_{4}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{\xi_{4}}{2}x_{2}u^{2}+x_{3}\xi_{4}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}=1.

Now, to count all the different functions among these notices how when
|\xi_{1}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}
we get

\xi_{1}x_{1}=\frac{\xi_{1}}{\xi_{4}}(\xi_{5}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{1})-\frac{\xi_{1}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}=-\frac{\xi_{1}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p},

so that we only need to count
\xi_{1}
when
\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and these correspond to the set
A_{3}
.

Case 3:
Now we have the representations indexed by the set
A_{4}
, which in this case have the property
|\xi_{3}|_{p}>|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}
, and they are all irreducible since

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,4}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{3}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\Big|\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{3}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{\xi_{3}^{2}x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}\xi_{3}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}^{2}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\xi_{3}|_{p}^{2}}{|\xi_{4}|_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{\xi_{3}^{2}x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}\xi_{3}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}^{2}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}=1,

where for the last equality we have used Lemma
2.4
.

Case 4:
