These are the representations indexed by
A_{5}
, and they have the property
|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{5}|_{p}\geq|\xi_{3}|_{p},
where

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,4}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2})\Big|\int_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{x_{2}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}x_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{\xi_{4}x_{2}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=|\xi_{4}|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\frac{\xi_{4}x_{2}}{2}u^{2}+(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2}x_{2}^{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3})u+\xi_{3}x_{2}u\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}=1,

where once again we apply Lemma
2.4
for the last equality.

Finally, notice how these are all the desired representations since:

\displaystyle\sum_{[\pi_{\xi}]\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,4}\cap\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\bot}}d_{\xi}^{2}=\sum_{\xi\in A_{1}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}d_{\xi}^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{2}\cup A_{3}\cup A_{4}\cup A_{5}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{\xi_{2}\in p^{-n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{1}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}(|\xi_{5}|_{p})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in p^{-n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}|\xi_{3}|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{3}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}^{2},

so that working out the expression we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{[\pi_{\xi}]\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,4}\cap\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\bot}}d_{\xi}^{2}=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}}p^{2n}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}p^{2n}|\xi_{4}|_{p}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}p^{2n}(p^{n}-|\xi_{4}|_{p})+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}p^{2n}|\xi_{5}|_{p}(p^{n}-|\xi_{5}|_{p})

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}<|\xi_{4}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}p^{3n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}p^{3n}|\xi_{5}|_{p}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{4n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}1=p^{5n}=|\mathbb{G}^{5,4}/\mathbb{G}^{5,4}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})|.

To finish our proof, we need to define the mappings

\delta_{\lambda}:L^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad\delta_{\lambda}(f)(u):=f(\lambda u).

Then, for
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
e can define the alternative representations

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})=\delta_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\circ\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\circ\delta_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}},

which act on the representation space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\spn_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}\,:\,\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\quad\varphi_{h}(u):=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u).

Explicitly, we can write

\displaystyle\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)

\displaystyle=\delta_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\circ\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\circ\delta_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\varphi(u)

\displaystyle=\delta_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\circ\pi_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}u)

\displaystyle=\delta_{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\circ e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(u+\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}))

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}u)\}_{p}}\varphi\big(p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}u)+\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}u)\}_{p}}\varphi\big(u+\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big),

so that with this realization the matrix coefficients are simply

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}))_{hh^{\prime}}=(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x},p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\big(\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big).

The proof is finished.
∎

We will use again our calculations of the unitary dual to describe the spectrum of the Vladimirov-sub-Laplacian, but this time it will be necessary to employ The following lemma, whose proof can be found in
[
1
]
:

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Phi:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be a parabolic ball–morphism which is continuously differentiable as function on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then it holds:

D^{\alpha}[f\circ\Phi](x)=|\Phi^{\prime}(x)|_{p}^{\alpha}D^{\alpha}[f]\circ\Phi(x).

Similar to the previous groups, for a collection
\textbf{W}:=\{W_{1},W_{2}\}
with

W_{i}=w_{i}^{1}X_{1}+w_{i}^{2}X_{2},

which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{5,4}/[\mathfrak{g}^{5,4},\mathfrak{g}^{5,4}]
, we can define its associated Vladimirov sub-Laplacian as

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}f(x):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{2}})f(\mathbf{x}),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4}).

For
\alpha>0
the following theorem proves how our conjecture for this operator holds true on
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
too. For a matter of simplicity, we will consider here only the case
\mathbf{W}=\{X_{1},X_{2}\}
, and we denote

\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}f(\mathbf{x}):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{X_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{X_{2}})f(\mathbf{x}),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4}),

but similar conclusions will hold for more general collections
\mathbf{W}
. The argument would be the same, but the calculations much longer.

Theorem 5.4
.

The Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}
is a globally hypoelliptic operator, which is invertible in the space of mean zero functions. Moreover, the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4})
can be written as the direct sum

L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,4})=\overline{\bigoplus_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,4}}\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}},\,\,\,\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

where each finite-dimensional sub-space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},

is an invariant sub-space of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, and the spectrum of
\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}}(\xi)
is composed by the numbers

|\xi_{1}+\tau+\frac{1}{2}\xi_{5}(h^{\prime})^{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}+\tau+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{\xi_{4}}h^{\prime}|_{p}^{\alpha}-2\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}},

where

1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p},\quad h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Proof.

In order to prove Theorem we want to use this calculate the associated symbol of
\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}
, and its respective invariant sub-spaces. Let us start by computing the symbols of the directional VT operators
\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha},\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}
,
\alpha>0
, by using what we got for the matrix coefficients. We only need to consider the case
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
.

We proved how

(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\frac{\xi_{4}x_{1}+\xi_{5}x_{2}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}),

and we know how each function
\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
has the Fourier series expansion

\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}e^{-2\pi i\{\tau(h^{\prime}-h)\}_{p}}e^{-2\pi i\{\tau u\}_{p}},

so that any
f\in\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}
can be written as

\displaystyle f(u)=\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

\displaystyle C_{h}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}\Big(\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1/2}e^{-2\pi i\{\tau(h^{\prime}-h)\}_{p}}C_{h}\Big)e^{-2\pi i\{\tau u\}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}(M_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{C}_{f})_{\tau}e^{2\pi i\{\tau u\}_{p}}.

In order to calculate the symbol of
\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}
, let us introduce some notation: let
\psi_{\xi}^{1}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{d_{\xi}\times d_{\xi}}
be the matrix function with entries

(\psi_{\xi}^{1})_{hh^{\prime}}(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}u\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\big(\frac{\xi_{4}u}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big),\,\,\,u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

The associated associated symbol
\sigma_{\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}\pi_{\xi}^{1}|_{x=0}
is a Toeplitz matrix which can be written as

\displaystyle\sigma_{\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=(\partial_{u}^{\alpha}\psi_{\xi}^{1}(u)|_{u=0})_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}\Big(\big|\xi_{1}+\frac{\xi_{4}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\times(M_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{C}_{f})_{\tau}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{1}+\tau\frac{\xi_{4}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}})u\}_{p}}|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}\Big(\xi_{1}+\big|\frac{\xi_{4}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\big|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)\times(M_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{C}_{f})_{\tau}.

Similarly, let
\psi_{\xi}^{2}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{d_{\xi}\times d_{\xi}}
be the matrix function with entries

\displaystyle(\psi_{\xi}^{2})_{hh^{\prime}}(u)

\displaystyle:=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}u^{3}+3\xi_{5}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(h^{\prime})^{2}u)+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}u)(h^{\prime})\}_{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\frac{\xi_{5}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}u),

where we use the following notation for short:
P_{\xi,\tau,h^{\prime}}(u):=

\xi_{2}u+\frac{\xi_{5}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}u+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}u^{3}+3\xi_{5}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(h^{\prime})^{2}u)+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}u^{2}+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}u)(h^{\prime}),

so that
\frac{d}{du}P_{\xi,\tau,h^{\prime}}(u)=

\xi_{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}+\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}2u+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(\xi_{5}^{3}3u^{2}+3\xi_{5}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(h^{\prime})^{2})+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(\frac{\xi_{5}}{2\xi_{4}}2u+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}})(h^{\prime}),

and

\frac{d}{du}P_{\xi,\tau,h^{\prime}}(u)|_{u=0}=\xi_{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(h^{\prime})^{2})+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}(h^{\prime})

The associated associated symbol
\sigma_{\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}\pi_{\xi}|_{x=0}
can be obtained as an application of

\displaystyle\sigma_{\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=(\partial_{u}^{\alpha}\psi_{\xi}^{2}(u)|_{u=0})_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}(M_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{C}_{f})_{\tau}\partial_{u}^{\alpha}(e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi,\tau,h^{\prime}}(u)\}_{p}})|_{u=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}}\Big(|\frac{d}{du}P_{\xi,\tau,h^{\prime}}(u)|_{u=0}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big)(M_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{C}_{f})_{\tau}.

This proves how, when
\xi\in A_{2}
, the spectrum of
\sigma_{\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}}(\xi)
is composed by the numbers

\displaystyle|\xi_{1}+

\displaystyle\frac{\xi_{4}}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}\tau|_{p}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+|\xi_{2}+\frac{\xi_{5}\tau}{p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}}+\frac{1}{6\xi_{4}\xi_{5}}(3\xi_{5}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}(h^{\prime})^{2})+p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{4}}(h^{\prime})|_{p}^{\alpha}-2\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}},

where

1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},\quad h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

In this way how

\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{op}=\max_{\tau,h^{\prime}}\lambda_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha})\lesssim\|\xi\|_{p}^{\alpha}.

In the same way

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{inf}

\displaystyle=\min_{\tau,h^{\prime}}\lambda_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha})\gtrsim|\xi_{1}|_{p}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}.

This implies that in each representation space we have the estimates

|\xi_{1}|_{p}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}\lesssim\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{inf}\leq\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{op}\lesssim\|\xi\|_{p}^{\alpha}.,

but we know that

\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{inf}=\|\sigma_{\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{inf},\,\,\,\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}}\|_{op}=\|\sigma_{\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}}(\xi)\|_{op},

proving how
\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}
provides an example of globally hypoelliptic operator.

This concludes the proof.

∎

6.
The group
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}

Let
p>3
be a prime number. In this section we consider the group
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, or just
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}
for simplicity, defined here as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
together with the non-commutative operation

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y}:=

\displaystyle(x_{1}+y_{1},x_{2}+y_{2},x_{3}+y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2},x_{4}+y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{2}+x_{1}y_{3},x_{5}+y_{5}+\frac{1}{6}x_{1}^{3}y_{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{3}+x_{1}y_{4}),

and associated inverse element

\mathbf{x}^{-1}=(-x_{1},-x_{2},-x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2},-x_{4}+x_{1}x_{3}-\frac{1}{2}x_{2}x_{1}^{2},-x_{5}+x_{1}x_{4}-\frac{1}{2}x_{3}x_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{6}x_{2}x_{1}^{3}).

We can identify this group with the exponential image of the filiform
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{5,5}
defined by the commutation relations

[X_{1},X_{2}]=X_{3},\quad[X_{1},X_{3}]=X_{4},\quad[X_{1},X_{4}]=X_{5}.

In the following theorem, we provide an explicit description of the unitary dual of this group.

Theorem 6.1
.

The unitary dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,5}
of the compact group
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}
can be identified with union of the following 2 disjoints subsets of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
:
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,5}=A_{1}\cup A_{2}
, where

A_{1}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p}\,\wedge\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1},\,\text{or}\,|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1\,\wedge\,\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

A_{2}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Moreover, each unitary irreducible representation can be realized in the finite dimensional Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\varphi_{h}:=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where
d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
, acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+ux_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u+x_{3}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}).

With this realization and the natural choice of basis for
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, the associated matrix coefficients are going to be

(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}(h^{\prime})+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+(h^{\prime})x_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h^{\prime})+x_{3}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1}),

and the associated characters are

\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{x_{2}(\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6})+x_{3}(\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2})+x_{4}(\xi_{5}u)\}_{p}}du.

Sometimes we will use the notation

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h,h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}\}.

Proof.

Once again, we can consider
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}
as an extension of the group
\mathcal{B}_{4}
, so the representation theory of
\mathbb{G}^{5,5}
should also include that of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
. The unitary irreducible representations of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
can be realized in the following finite dimensional sub-space
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
of
L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
:

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,\,:\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

where
d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\max\{|\xi_{3}|_{p},|\xi_{4}|_{p}\}
, and the representation acts on functions
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}x_{1}+\xi_{2}x_{2}+\xi_{3}(x_{3}+ux_{2})+\xi_{4}(x_{4}+ux_{3}+\frac{u^{2}}{2}x_{2})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}).

On the other hand, if a representation is not trivial on the variable
x_{5}
, then we must have
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
, and we can realize these in the finite dimensional Hilbert spaces

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(u)\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},\,\,d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p},

acting according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+ux_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u+x_{3}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}).

Let us write

P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u):=\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+ux_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u+x_{3}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6}).

Then we can see how
\pi_{\xi}
is indeed a representation since:

\displaystyle P_{\xi}(\textbf{y},u+x_{1})=

\displaystyle\xi\cdot\textbf{y}+\xi_{3}y_{2}(u+x_{1})+\xi_{4}(y_{2}\frac{(u+x_{1})^{2}}{2}+(u+x_{1})y_{3})+\xi_{5}(y_{4}(u+x_{1})+y_{3}\frac{(u+x_{1})^{2}}{2}+y_{2}\frac{(u+x_{1})^{3}}{6})

\displaystyle=\xi_{1}y_{1}+\xi_{2}y_{2}+\xi_{3}(y_{3}x_{1}y_{2})+\xi_{4}((y_{4}+x_{1}\frac{y_{2}^{2}}{2}+x_{1}y_{3})+y_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+(y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})u)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,+\xi_{5}((y_{5}+\frac{x_{1}^{3}}{6}y_{3}+\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2}y_{3}+x_{1}y_{4})+(y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})\frac{u^{2}}{2}+(y_{4}+\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2}y_{2}+x_{1}y_{3})u+y_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6}),

and thus it clearly holds

P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}(\textbf{y},u+x_{1})=P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y},u).

From this we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}(\textbf{y})\varphi(u)=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})(e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\textbf{y},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+y_{1}))

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}(\textbf{y},u+x_{1})\}_{p}}\varphi(u+x_{1}+y_{1})

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})_{1})

\displaystyle=\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y})\varphi(u).

It is not hard to see how this representation defines an unitary operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
. By using the natural choice of basis for this space, we can compute

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}))_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(x,u)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h}(u+x_{1})\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\,\,\quad\times\int_{h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{3}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+ux_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u+x_{3}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}(h^{\prime})+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+(h^{\prime})x_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h^{\prime})+x_{3}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1}).

From this it readily follows that

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in I_{\xi}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+K_{\xi}}(x_{1})\int_{h^{\prime}+K_{\xi}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{3}x_{2}u+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+ux_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u+x_{3}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{u^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{x_{2}(\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6})+x_{3}(\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2})+x_{4}(\xi_{5}u)\}_{p}}du.

We can use the above function with two purposes: first to check how these representations are indeed irreducible, and second, we can also count the number of different non-equivalent classes of unitary irreducible representations, by counting the number of different functions among these characters. Let us start by checking how these representations are indeed irreducible. With this end in mind, let us define

f_{\xi}(x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}):=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{x_{2}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6})+x_{3}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2})+x_{4}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u)\}_{p}}du.

Then, computing its Fourier transform as an element of
L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}

\displaystyle[f](\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{x_{2}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha)+x_{3}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)+x_{4}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma)\}_{p}}dudx_{2}dx_{3}dx_{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma)du.

Now let
A_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
be the intersection of the sets

\{u:\,\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\,\{u:\,\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},\,\{u:\,\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}

We know that

•

h_{1}(u):=\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha
satisfies
h_{1}(u+v)=h_{1}(u)
for
|v|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}.

•

h_{2}(u):=\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta
satisfies
h_{2}(u+v)=h_{2}(u)
for
|v|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}.

•

h_{3}(u):=\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma
satisfies
h_{3}(u+v)=h_{3}(u)
for
|v|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{-1}.

In this way, we can think on the intersection
A_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
as a ball of radius
\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}
whenever the intersection is non-empty. So, if we define
\mathbf{N}_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
as the either
1
or
0
, depending on whether the intersection is non-empty, we have
N_{\xi}=N_{\xi}^{2}
and

\displaystyle\|f_{\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}|\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}[f_{\xi}](\alpha,\beta,\gamma)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}N_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)^{2}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-2}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}N_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1},

where the last equality holds because

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}N_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma)du=1.

Next we need to count our representations. Here we consider again cases. First, assume
|\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
, and take
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
|\theta|_{p}=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}
. Notice how

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}[f_{\xi}](\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in I_{\xi}}\int_{a+K_{\xi}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma)du

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in I_{\xi}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}a+\xi_{4}\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{a^{3}}{6}-\alpha)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}a+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}a-\gamma)du

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in I_{\xi}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}\mathds{1}_{d_{\xi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\theta(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}a+\xi_{4}\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{a^{3}}{6}-\alpha))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\mathds{1}_{d_{\xi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\theta(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}a+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta))\mathds{1}_{d_{\xi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\theta(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}a-\gamma))du

\displaystyle=\textbf{N}_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1},

where
\textbf{N}_{\xi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
equals the number of solutions in
I_{\xi}
to the system of equations

•

Q_{1}(u):=\theta(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}u+\xi_{4}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{3}}{6}-\alpha)\equiv 0\,\,\mathrm{mod}\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

•

Q_{2}(u):=\theta(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}u+\xi_{5}\frac{u^{2}}{2}-\beta)\equiv 0\,\,\mathrm{mod}\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

•

Q_{3}(u):=\theta(\xi_{4}+\xi_{5}u-\gamma)\equiv 0\,\,\mathrm{mod}\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

Using Hensel’s lemma, we can see how for every fixed
\gamma
there is always at most one solution
u_{\gamma}
for a single pair
(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, so
\textbf{N}_{\xi}=1
and we just proved how the Fourier transform of
f
is independent of
\xi_{2}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
if
|\xi_{2}|_{p}
. In the case
|\xi_{2}|_{p}>\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
, the only way these three equations have a solution is that
|\alpha-\xi_{2}|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}
. This shows how this time
f_{\xi}
does depend on
\xi_{2}
, and we only need to consider
\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Finally, to check how these are all the desired representations, just notice how

\displaystyle\sum_{\{[\pi_{\xi}]\,:\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}\}}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{[\pi_{\xi}]\,:\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1\}}d_{\xi}^{2}+\sum_{\{[\pi_{\xi}]\,:\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n},\,\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1\}}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{1\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{2n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{1\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}1

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{4n}(p^{n}-1)=p^{5n}=|\mathbb{G}^{5,5}/\mathbb{G}^{5,5}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})|.

This concludes the proof.
∎

With the above theorem, functions
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,5})
have the Fourier series representation

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}+\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}|\xi_{3}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{\,|\xi_{4}|_{p}>|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1=|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{\xi_{2}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}|\xi_{4}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(x)\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)],

where the Fourier transform
\widehat{f}(\xi)
is the linear operator defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle(\xi)\varphi(u):=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,5}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}(u-x_{1})+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{(u-x_{1})^{2}}{2}+(u-x_{1})x_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(u-x_{1})+x_{3}\frac{(u-x_{1})^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{(u-x_{1})^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\varphi(u-x_{1})d\mathbf{x},\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}.

Alternatively, by using our expressions for the matrix coefficients we can see this operator as the matrix
\widehat{f}(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}^{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}\times\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}}
defined by the expression

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,5}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})_{hh^{\prime}}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}(h)+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{(h)^{2}}{2}+(h)x_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h)+x_{3}\frac{(h)^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{(h)^{3}}{6})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})d\mathbf{x},

where
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, which in terms of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
-Fourier transform is expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+h_{1}+\xi_{5}(\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}+h_{1}),\xi_{3}+\xi_{5}h_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}]*_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[f](\xi_{1},\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}h+\xi_{4}\frac{h^{2}}{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{h^{3}}{6},\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}h+\xi_{5}\frac{h^{2}}{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{5}).

Given any collection
\textbf{W}:=\{W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}
} with

W_{i}=w_{i}^{1}X_{1}+w_{i}^{2}X_{2}+w_{i}^{3}X_{3},

which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{5,5}/[\mathfrak{g}^{5,5},\mathfrak{g}^{5,5}]
, we can define its associated Vladimirov sub-Laplacian as

\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}f(x):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{2}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{W_{3}})f(x),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,5}).

For
\alpha>0
the following is the spectral theorem corresponding to this operator .

Theorem 6.2
.

The Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
associated with the collection
W
is a globally hypoelliptic operator, which is invertible in the space of mean zero functions. Moreover, the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,5})
can be written as the direct sum

L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,5})=\overline{\bigoplus_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,5}}\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}},\,\,\,\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

where each finite-dimensional sub-space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},

is an invariant sub-space of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, and its spectrum restricted to
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}
is given by

Spec(\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h}})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}|(\xi_{1}+\tau,\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}h^{\prime}+\xi_{4}\frac{1}{2}(h^{\prime})^{2}+\xi_{5}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{3}}{6},\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}h^{\prime}+\xi_{5}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2})\cdot W_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-3\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}},

and the corresponding eigenfunctions are given by

\mathscr{e}_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{x}):=e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}(h^{\prime})+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+(h^{\prime})x_{3})+\xi_{5}(x_{4}(h^{\prime})+x_{3}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+x_{2}\frac{(h^{\prime})^{3}}{6})+\tau x_{1}\}_{p}},

where
1\leq|\tau|_{p}\leq\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}.

7.
The group
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}

Let
p>3
be a prime number. In this final section we consider the group
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, or just
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}
for simplicity, defined here as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
together with the non-commutative operation

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y}:=

\displaystyle(x_{1}+y_{1})X_{1}+(x_{2}+y_{2})X_{2}+(x_{3}+y_{3}+x_{1}y_{2})X_{3}

\displaystyle+(x_{4}+y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}y_{2}+x_{1}y_{3})X_{4}+(x_{5}+y_{5}+\frac{1}{6}x_{1}^{3}y_{3}+x_{1}y_{4}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}y_{2}^{2}+x_{2}y_{3}+x_{1}x_{2}y_{2})X_{5},

and associated inverse element

\mathbf{x}^{-1}=(-x_{1},-x_{2},-x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2},-x_{4}+x_{1}x_{3}-\frac{1}{2}x_{2}x_{1}^{2},-x_{5}+x_{1}x_{4}+x_{2}x_{3}-\frac{1}{2}x_{3}x_{1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{1}+\frac{1}{6}x_{2}x_{1}^{3}).

We can identify this group with the exponential image of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{5,6}
defined by the commutation relations

[X_{1},X_{2}]=X_{3},\quad[X_{1},X_{3}]=X_{4},\quad[X_{1},X_{4}]=X_{5},\quad[X_{2},X_{3}]=X_{5}.

In the following theorem, we provide an explicit description of the unitary dual of this group, which is actually the last one in the list of
5
-dimensional groups which we intend to consider in this work. But before that, we wil need to make use of the following lemma:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
, and take
\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u+\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u^{2}}{2}\}_{p}}du\Big|^{2}dx_{2}dx_{3}dx_{4}=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{-1}.

Proof.

Same arguments as in Lemma
2.3
.
∎

Theorem 7.2
.

The unitary dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,6}
of
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}
can be identified with the union of the following two disjoint subsets of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
:
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,6}=A_{1}\cup A_{2}
, where

A_{1}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1\leq|\xi_{4}|_{p}<|\xi_{3}|_{p}\,\wedge\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1},\,\text{or}\,|\xi_{3}|_{p}=1\,\wedge\,\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\},

A_{2}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1,\,(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

Moreover, if we denote
K_{\xi}:=p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
I_{\xi}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}/K_{\xi}
, each unitary irreducible representation can be realized in the finite dimensional Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}^{1/2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{h+K_{\xi}}(u)\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},\,\,d_{\xi}:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}.

If we define the polynomials

\displaystyle P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)

\displaystyle:=\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}+\xi_{3}x_{2}u_{1}+\xi_{4}(x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+u_{1}x_{3})+\xi_{5}\big(\frac{1}{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{1}+\frac{1}{6}x_{2}x_{1}^{3}+x_{3}u_{2}+x_{1}x_{2}u_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}x_{1}^{3}u_{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}u_{2}^{2}+x_{4}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{2}x_{1}^{2}u_{1}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}x_{2}u_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{3}u_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}u_{1}u_{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}u_{2}u_{1}^{2}\big)

then the representations act according to the formula

\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u):=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u+(x_{1},x_{2})).

With this realization and the natural choice of basis for
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, the associated matrix coefficients are going to be

(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2}),

and the associated characters are
\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})=

\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}.

Sometimes we will use the notation

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h,h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}\}.

Proof.

First, we consider the representations which are trivial on
H=\textbf{exp}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}X_{5})
. Since
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}/H\cong\mathcal{B}_{4}
we can also consider
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}
as an extension of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
, so the representation theory of
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}
should also include that of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
. See again Theorem
2.6
and the full calculations in
[
28
]
. On the other hand, if a representation is not trivial on the variable
x_{5}
, we must have
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
, and we can see how
\pi_{\xi}
is indeed a representation since
P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)+P_{\xi}(\textbf{y},u+(x_{1},x_{2}))=P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}\star\textbf{y},u)
. This defines an unitary operator and, by using the natural choice of basis for this space, we can compute

\displaystyle(\pi_{\xi}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{x}))_{hh^{\prime}}=(\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\varphi_{h},\varphi_{h^{\prime}})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(x,u)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h}(u+(x_{1},x_{2}))\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\int_{h^{\prime}+K_{\xi}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h^{\prime})\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2}).

From this it readily follows that

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in I_{\xi}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{K_{\xi}}(x_{1},x_{2})\times\int_{h+K_{\xi}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})\times\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})\times\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du.

We can use the function with two purposes. First to check how these representations are indeed irreducible, and second, we can also count the number of different non-equivalent classes of unitary irreducible representations, by counting the number of different functions among these characters. Let us start by noticing how, if
1<\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\leq|\xi_{5}|_{p},
then we get

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(x_{3},x_{4}),

so that all of these
\pi_{\xi}
are irreducible but equivalent to the same
\pi_{\xi}
with
|\xi_{3}|_{p}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}=1
. In the case
|\xi_{5}|_{p}<\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}
we get

\displaystyle\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}}(x_{2})\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{3}x_{2}u_{1}+\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}u_{1}+\xi_{5}(x_{4}u_{1}+x_{3}u_{2})\}_{p}}du

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}.

By making a standard change of variable we can see how

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,4}}|\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}^{2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}}(x_{1},)\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}}(x_{2})\mathds{1}_{p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{3})\big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}\big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}^{2}|\xi_{5}|_{p}^{2}p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}p^{2\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}\big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}\big|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{4}x_{2}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{2}+(\xi_{3}x_{2}+\xi_{4}x_{3}+\xi_{5}x_{4})u_{1}\}_{p}}du_{1}\big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}=1,

where the last equality follows from Lemma
7.1
. In other words,
\pi_{\xi}
is irreducible. Finally, it is easy to see how the list of different functions among the characters
\chi_{\pi_{\xi}}
can be indexed by:

•

If
|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1
, then
\pi_{\xi}
must descend to a representation of
\mathcal{B}_{4}
, so these are indexed by:

A_{1}=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{4}\}.

•

If
|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1
then the different characters can be indexed by

A_{2}:=\{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}\,:1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\,(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\}.

These are all the desired representations since:

\displaystyle\sum_{[\pi_{\xi}]\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,5}}

\displaystyle d_{\xi}^{2}=\sum_{\xi\in A_{1}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}d_{\xi}^{2}+\sum_{\xi\in A_{2}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{p}\leq p^{n}}d_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}(\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p})^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{4n}+p^{4n}\sum_{1<|\xi_{5}|_{p}\leq p^{n}}1

\displaystyle=p^{5n}=|\mathbb{G}^{5,6}/\mathbb{G}^{5,6}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})|.

This concludes the proof.
∎

According to Theorem
7.2
, functions
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,6})
have the Fourier series representation

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}+\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{4}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{-1}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\widehat{f}(\xi)],

where the Fourier transform
\widehat{f}(\xi)
is the linear operator defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)\varphi(u)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,6}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\varphi(u)d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{2\pi i\{P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x}^{-1},u)\}_{p}}\varphi(u-(x_{1},x_{2})),d\mathbf{x},\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}.

Alternatively, if we define

\tilde{P}_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u):=P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},u)-\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}

by using our expressions for the matrix coefficients we can see this operator as the matrix
\widehat{f}(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}^{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}\times\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}}
defined by the expression

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{5,6}}f(\mathbf{x})\pi_{\xi}^{*}(\mathbf{x})_{hh^{\prime}}d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{2\pi i\{-P_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})d\mathbf{x},

where
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
, which in terms of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}
-Fourier transform is expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[e^{2\pi i\{-\tilde{P}_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(x_{1},x_{2})f](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[e^{2\pi i\{-\tilde{P}_{\xi}(\mathbf{x},h)\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\cdot,\cdot)]*_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{5}}[f](\xi).

With this Fourier series representation, a linear invariant operator
T
can be written as a pseudo-differential operator

\displaystyle T_{\sigma}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}=1}\sigma(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot\mathbf{x}\}_{p}}+\sum_{|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\,\,(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{4}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\sigma(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\,|\xi_{5}|_{p}>1}\sum_{(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{-1}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2}/p^{\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})\|_{p}|\xi_{5}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(\mathbf{x})\sigma(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)],

To conclude this section we are interested once again in studying the associated Vladimirov sub-Laplacian as

\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}f(x):=(\partial^{\alpha}_{X_{1}}+\partial^{\alpha}_{X_{2}})f(x),\,\,\,f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{G}^{5,6}).

For
\alpha>0
the following theorem proves how our conjecture for this operator holds true in the last one of the
5
-dimensional groups
\mathbb{G}^{5,6}
.

Theorem 7.3
.

The Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathcal{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}
is a globally hypoelliptic operator, which is invertible in the space of mean zero functions. Moreover, the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,6})
can be written as the direct sum

L^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{5,6})=\overline{\bigoplus_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{5,6}}\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}},\,\,\,\mathcal{V}_{\xi}=\bigoplus_{h^{\prime}\in I_{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}},

where each finite-dimensional sub-space

\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{(\pi_{\xi})_{hh^{\prime}}\,:\,h\in I_{\xi}\},

is an invariant sub-space of
\mathcal{L}_{sub,\textbf{W}}^{\alpha}
, and when restricted to
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}
we get the estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{h^{\prime}}}\|_{inf}

\displaystyle=\min_{\tau,h^{\prime}}\lambda_{\xi,h^{\prime},\tau}(\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha})\gtrsim|\xi_{1}|_{p}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}.

Proof.

As we anticipated, we will be following the same arguments as in Theorem
3.4
. In order to prove Theorem
7.3
we want to use this calculate the associated symbol of
\mathscr{L}_{sub}^{\alpha}
, and its respective invariant sub-spaces. Let us start by computing the symbols of the directional VT operators
\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha},\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}
,
\alpha>0
, by using what we got for the matrix coefficients. For
\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}
, let us introduce some notation: let
\psi_{\xi}^{1}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{d_{\xi}\times d_{\xi}}
be the matrix function with entries

(\psi_{\xi}^{1})_{hh^{\prime}}(t):=\delta_{h_{2}^{\prime}h_{2}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{1}+\xi_{5}(\frac{(h_{2}^{\prime})^{2}}{2}+\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}(h_{2}^{\prime})}{2}))t+\frac{\xi_{5}(h_{1}^{\prime})(h_{2}^{\prime})}{2}t^{2}+\xi_{5}(\frac{h_{2}^{\prime}}{6}+\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}{2})t^{3}\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h_{1}^{\prime}-h_{1}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(t),\,\,\,u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

The associated associated symbol
\sigma_{\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}\pi_{\xi}|_{\mathbf{x}=0}
is a matrix which can be written as

\sigma_{\partial_{X_{1}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}|\xi_{1}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\quad&\text{if}\quad|\xi_{3}|_{p}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}=|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\\
\partial^{\alpha}_{t}(e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}t\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h^{\prime}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}})|_{t=0}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1=|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}>1,\,\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\\
\partial^{\alpha}_{t}(\psi_{\xi}^{1})_{hh^{\prime}})|_{t=0}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,h\in p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\end{cases}

where
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}
is the VT operator

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\psi(t):=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{\psi(t-v)-\psi(t)}{|v|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dv,\,\,\,\,\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

In a similar way, for
\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}
let us introduce the

(\psi_{\xi}^{2})_{hh^{\prime}}(t):=\delta_{h_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}}e^{2\pi i\{(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}(h_{1}^{\prime})+\xi_{4}\frac{(h_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}{2})t\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h_{2}^{\prime}-h_{2}+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(t),\,\,\,u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

The associated associated symbol
\sigma_{\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}\pi_{\xi}|_{\mathbf{x}=0}
is a matrix which can be written as

\sigma_{\partial_{X_{2}}^{\alpha}}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}|\xi_{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\quad&\text{if}\quad|\xi_{3}|_{p}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}=|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\\
\big(|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}h+\xi_{4}\frac{h^{2}}{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\big)\delta_{hh^{\prime}}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1=|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p}>1,\,\,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\\
\partial^{\alpha}_{t}(\psi_{\xi}^{2})_{hh^{\prime}})|_{t=0}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1<|\xi_{5}|_{p},\,\,h\in p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{5})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.\end{cases}

Summing up, the symbol of the Vladimirov Sub-Laplacian
\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}_{sub}
is component-wise
\sigma_{\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}_{sub}}(\xi)_{hh^{\prime}}=

\begin{cases}|\xi_{1}|_{p}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}-2\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\quad&\text{if}\quad|\xi_{3}|_{p}=|\xi_{4}|_{p}=|\xi_{5}|_{p}=1,\\
(\partial_{u}^{\alpha}+|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}h+\xi_{4}\frac{h^{2}}{2}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}})(e^{2\pi i\{\xi_{1}u\}_{p}}\mathds{1}_{h^{\prime}-h+p^{-\vartheta(\xi_{3})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}})(u))|_{u=0},\quad&\text{if}\quad 1=|\xi_{5}|_{p}<\|(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{p},\\
\partial^{\alpha}_{t}(\psi_{\xi}^{1})_{hh^{\prime}})|_{t=0}+\partial^{\alpha}_{t}(\psi_{\xi}^{2})_{hh^{\prime}})|_{t=0},\quad&\text{if}\quad 1<|\xi_{5}|.\end{cases}

∎

8.
Final remarks

8.1.
Relation to non-invariant vector fields on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}

One of the main advantages of invariant operators is how, using the representation theory of the group, one can construct easily a fundamental solution in terms of the associated symbol, just like in Rockland’s seminal paper
[
25
]
. To give an example, in the particular case when
\mathbb{G}=\mathfrak{g}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
, the directional VT operators introduced in Definition
1.4
take the form

\partial_{V}^{\alpha}f(x):=\frac{1-p^{\alpha}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}}\frac{f(x-tV(x))-f(x)}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt,

where
V:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
is an analytic vector field. For this operator, one can easily compute its associated symbol:

\sigma_{\partial_{V}^{\alpha}}(x,\xi)=\begin{cases}0,\,&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|V(x)\cdot\xi|_{p}\leq 1,\\
|V(x)\cdot\xi|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}&\,\,\text{if}\,\,|V(x)\cdot\xi|_{p}>1.\end{cases}

With the above calculation it is easy to check how the Vladimirov Laplacian
\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
is a globally hypoelliptic operator with symbol

\sigma_{\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}}(\xi)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Big(|\xi_{i}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}\Big),\quad\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{d},

and as a direct consequence we can construct a fundamental solution for the operator given by the following convolution kernel:

E_{\alpha}(x)=\sum_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{d}}\sigma_{\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\{\xi\cdot x\}_{p}}.

When the vector field
V
is not constant, constructing a fundamental solution becomes more complicated, but there are some interesting cases where it is actually possible to obtain it explicitly. For instance, on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
, consider the directional VT operator in the direction of the vector field

V(x,y,z)=e_{2}+xe_{3}=(0,1,x),

defined via the formula

\displaystyle\partial_{V}^{\alpha}f(x,y,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{f((x,y,z)+tV(x,y,z))-f(x,y,z)}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{f(x,y+t,z+tx)-f(x,y,z)}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt.

From the perspective of the Fourier analysis on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
, the resulting operator is non-invariant, and its associated symbol, corresponding to the Fourier transform on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2d+1}
is going to be

\sigma_{\partial_{V}^{\alpha}}(\gamma)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{e^{2\pi i\{\gamma\cdot t(0,1,x)\}_{p}}-1}{|t|_{p}^{\alpha+1}}dt=|\gamma\cdot(0,1,x)|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}=|\gamma_{2}+x\gamma_{3}|_{p}^{\alpha}-\frac{1-p^{-1}}{1-p^{-(\alpha+1)}}.

With this symbolic representation it is not trivial to check the globall hypoellipticity of the operator, but fortunately we can use an alternative perspective. If instead we consider
f
as a function on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
, which is simply
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
with a different operation, then the operator
\partial_{V}^{\alpha}
is the VT directional operator associated to
Y\in\mathfrak{h}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, which is invariant and therefore a Fourier multiplier with the group Fourier analysis of
\mathbb{H}_{1}
. Actually, if we consider an operator like
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}+\partial_{V}^{\alpha}
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{3}
, then by using the group Fourier transform we can check how it coincides with the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian
\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}_{sub}
on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
, for which we can construct explicitly a fundamental solution in terms of the
\star
-convolution kernel

E_{\alpha}(x,y,z)=\sum_{\xi\in\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1}}|\xi_{3}|_{p}Tr[\pi_{\xi}(x,y,z)\sigma_{\mathscr{L}^{\alpha}_{sub}}^{-1}(\xi)].

8.2.
Future research

There are several things we can argue from the results obtained in this work. More importantly, our analysis here opens several questions that I think are worth to be explored.

(1)

As we saw before, in every case the unitary dual can be thought as a certain ”sub-tree” of the full tree
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{5}
. So, the guess is that, at least for the nilpotent case, in general dimension
d:=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{G})>5
the unitary dual of
\mathbb{G}^{5,4}
should be something similar, a sort of pruned version of the tree
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{d}
.

(2)

It is not clear to me how to generate the representations for arbitrary groups. I know some works on representation theory and the orbit method for some kinds of profinite groups but, to the best knowledge of the author, nothing to produce something like the matrix coefficients in the general case, just the characters. Ideally we would have the matrix coefficients to perform Fourier analysis better, as the case of the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian illustrates.

(3)

A question I would like to answer is whether this operator satisfies some sub-elliptic estimates.

(4)

The definition of directional VT operators works the same on non-compact groups, where at least in the graded case one can also study the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian. In that setting one also has homogeneous structures and homogeneous operators very similar to Rockland operators.

(5)

Another important question I would like to ask is whether one can find a nice expression for the heat kernel of the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian, maybe in a similar way as in
[
2
]
.

(6)

Our analysis here does not include any information about the multiplicity of the eigenvalues of the Vladimirov sub-Laplacian. We want to remark how, specially in the case of the Heisenberg group, this operator acts like some kind of
p
-adic Schrodinger operator, like the ones studied in the reference book
[
30
]
.

J.P. Velasquez-Rodriguez,
Departamento de Matematicas, Universidad del Valle, Cali-Colombia

E-mail address:

velasquez.juan@correounivalle.edu.co
